\gr V_{x}\;=\;\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}}V_{x}^{(\alpha)}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{where}\hskip 10.00002ptV_{x}^{(\alpha)}\;=\;V_{x}^{\alpha}/V_{x}^{<\alpha}.

By construction the filtration is preserved by the stabilizer
Q_{x}\subset G
of
x
. Recall that this stabilizer may be disconnected; its connected component is the parabolic subgroup
Q_{x}^{\circ}=Q_{x}\cap G^{\circ}
of
G^{\circ}

[
Mar03
, prop. 5.2]
. Let
\rad Q_{x}
be the unipotent radical of this connected component; the associated Levi quotient
Q_{x}^{\textup{ss}}=Q_{x}/\rad Q_{x}
faithfully acts on
\gr V_{x}
in a way preserving each graded piece, i.e. we have a natural embedding

\varepsilon_{x}\colon\hskip 10.00002ptQ_{x}^{\textup{ss}}\;\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\;\prod_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}}\GL(V_{x}^{(\alpha)}).

For a finite subset
I\subset\mathbb{Q}
we consider the graded vector space
\gr_{I}V_{x}:=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in I}V_{x}^{(\alpha)}
and denote by

\varepsilon_{x,I}\colon\hskip 10.00002ptQ_{x}^{\textup{ss}}\;\longrightarrow\;\gr_{I}\GL(V_{x})\;:=\;\prod_{\alpha\in I}\GL(V_{x}^{(\alpha)}),

the composite of the morphism
\varepsilon_{x}
with the projection to the factors that are indexed by the subset
I
. For
x,y\in\mathcal{B}(G,k)
and
I\subset\mathbb{Q}
, let

\gr_{I}\Tran_{\GL(V)}(x,y)\subset\Iso(\gr_{I}V_{x},\gr_{I}V_{y})

be the closed subvariety of all graded linear isomorphisms
f\colon\gr_{I}V_{x}\to\gr_{I}V_{y}
such that

f(\im\varepsilon_{x,I})f^{-1}=\im\varepsilon_{y,I}.

The stabilizer
Q_{x}
acts freely on the transporter
\Tran_{G}(x,y)
by right multiplication.

Lemma 3.16
.

If
\varepsilon_{x,I}
and
\varepsilon_{y,I}
are injective, then the morphism

\Tran_{G}(x,y)\longrightarrow\gr_{I}\Tran_{\GL(V)}(x,y),\hskip 10.00002ptg\longmapsto\gr_{I}g

is invariant under the action of
\rad Q_{x}
on the source and factors through a closed immersion

\Tran_{G}(x,y)/\rad Q_{x}\;\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\;\gr_{I}\Tran_{\GL(V)}(x,y).

Proof.

Immediate from the definitions.
∎

In particular, if the morphism
\Tran_{G}(x,y)\to\gr_{I}\Tran_{\GL(V)}(x,y)
is surjective, then it is a principal bundle under
\rad Q_{x}
and therefore surjective on
k
-points by vanishing of the first Galois cohomology group of smooth connected unipotent groups over perfect fields.

Definition 3.17
.

If
k
is algebraically closed, we say that the subgroup
G\subset\GL(V)
is
full
if for every
x,y\in\mathcal{B}(G,k)
that are conjugate under
\GL(V)(k)
, there exists a subset
I\subset\mathbb{Q}
with the property that
\varepsilon_{x,I}
and
\varepsilon_{y,I}
are embeddings and the natural morphism

\Tran_{G}(x,y)\longrightarrow\gr_{I}\Tran_{\GL(V)}(x,y)

is surjective. If
k
is arbitrary, we say that the subgroup
G\subset\GL(V)
is
full
if it becomes a full subgroup after extending scalars to an algebraic closure of
k
.

As we will see below in
example

3.19
it is necessary to have
I\neq\mathbb{Q}
in order to allow orthogonal and symplectic groups to be full.

Example 3.18
.

Taking
x=y\in\mathcal{B}(G,k)
to be the trivial cocharacter and looking at the action scalar matrices, one sees that the determinant
\det\colon\GL(V)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
must restrict to a surjective character on every full subgroup. In particular the subgroup
G=\SL(V)\subset\GL(V)
is not full, although
G=\GL(V)
is full.

Example 3.19
.

Let
\theta
be a nondegenerate symmetric or alternating bilinear form on
V
, and let

G\;=\;\begin{cases}\GSp(V,\theta)&\text{if $\theta$ is alternating},\\
\GO(V,\theta)&\text{if $\theta$ is symmetric}.\end{cases}

Then
G\subset\GL(V)
is a full subgroup. Indeed, by definition we may assume that
k
is algebraically closed. Since any parabolic subgroup of
G^{\circ}
is the stabilizer of an isotropic flag, up to relabelling indices the filtration induced by any
x\in\mathcal{B}(G,k)
has the form

0=V_{x}^{0}\subset V_{x}^{1}\subset V_{x}^{2}\subset\cdots\subset V_{x}^{r}\subset(V_{x}^{r})^{\perp}\subset\cdots\subset(V_{x}^{1})^{\perp}\subset(V_{x}^{0})^{\perp}=V

with isotropic subspaces
V^{i}_{x}\subset V
and
r=r(x)
depending on
x
. Here we allow that
(V_{x}^{r})^{\perp}=V_{x}^{r}
but all the other inclusions in the above filtration are assumed to be strict. On the quotient
U_{x}:=(V_{x}^{r})^{\perp}/V_{x}^{r}
the bilinear form
\theta
induces a nondegenerate bilinear form
\theta_{x}
which is symmetric or alternating if
\theta
is so, and we put

H_{x}\;:=\;\begin{cases}\GSp(U_{x},\theta_{x})&\text{if $\theta$ is alternating},\\
\GO(U_{x},\theta_{x})&\text{if $\theta$ is symmetric}.\end{cases}

Put

W_{x}^{i}:=V_{x}^{i}/V_{x}^{i-1}
for
i=1,\dots,r
.
Then

\gr V_{x}\;=\;W_{x}^{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus W_{x}^{r}\oplus U_{x}\oplus(W_{x}^{r})^{\vee}\oplus\cdots\oplus(W_{x}^{1})^{\vee}

where by construction each direct summand sits in a different graded piece, and the Levi quotient

Q_{x}^{\textup{ss}}\;=\;\GL(W_{x}^{1})\times\cdots\times\GL(W_{x}^{r})\times H_{x}

acts on
\gr V_{x}
in the natural way preserving the grading. If we choose
I\subset\mathbb{Q}
such that

\gr_{I}V_{x}\;=\;W_{x}^{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus W_{x}^{r}\oplus U_{x}

is the direct sum of the first half of the graded pieces including the middle piece, then

\varepsilon_{x,I}\colon\hskip 10.00002ptQ_{x}^{\textup{ss}}\;\hookrightarrow\;\gr_{I}\GL(V_{x})\;=\;\GL(W_{x}^{1})\times\cdots\times\GL(W_{x}^{r})\times\GL(U_{x})

is an embedding because the action on the remaining graded pieces is determined by duality. Now
x,y\in\mathcal{B}(G,k)
be two points conjugate under
\GL(V)(k)
. Then in particular the associated filtrations have the same length, so they are of the above shape with
r=r(x)=r(y)
. Then by definition any point of
\gr_{I}\Tran_{\GL(V)}(x,y)
is a direct sum
\bar{g}=\bar{g}_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\bar{g}_{r}\oplus\bar{h}
of isomorphisms

\bar{g}_{i}\colon\hskip 10.00002ptW_{x}^{i}\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\;W_{y}^{i}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\bar{h}\colon\hskip 10.00002ptU_{x}\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\;U_{y}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{with}\hskip 10.00002pt\bar{h}H_{x}\bar{h}^{-1}=H_{y}.

Now by Witt’s theorem
[
Art57
, th. III.3.9]
, there exists an element
g\in G(k)
such that

•

g(V_{x}^{i})\;=\;V_{y}^{i}
and hence also
g((V_{x}^{i})^{\perp})=(V_{y}^{i})^{\perp}
for
i=1,\dots,r
, and

•

g
induces the given isomorphisms
\bar{g}_{1},\dots,\bar{g}_{r},\bar{h}
between graded pieces.

Then
g\in\Tran_{G}(x,y)
lifts the element
\bar{g}\in\gr_{I}\Tran_{\GL(V)}(x,y)
as required.

Note that it is essential that we used only half of the graded pieces. To make it simple suppose
x=y
so we can drop the subscript
x
in the notation above. Assume moreover that the filtration associated with
x
is made of one single Lagrangian subspace
V^{1}=W
. In this case

\varepsilon\colon Q^{\textup{ss}}=\GL(W)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\GL(W)\times\GL(W^{\vee}).

The normalizer of
Q^{\textup{ss}}
in
\GL(W)\times\GL(W^{\vee})
is strictly larger than
Q^{\textup{ss}}
as it contains the center
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Composing
\varepsilon
with the projection onto one of the two factors of
\GL(W)\times\GL(W^{\vee})
permits to get rid of this problem.

4.
Realizations with extra structures

In this section we introduce the étale and de Rham realizations to which the Lawrence-Venkatesh method will be applied, that we call respectively
étale data
and
de Rham data
. A particular accent is put on the extra symmetries that these data satisfy, as they will be crucial for the method to work. We formulate Faltings’ finiteness theorem in the context of semisimple pure étale data and discuss the effect of semisimplification on de Rham data. Let
k
be a field of characteristic
0
and
p
a prime number.

4.1.
Semilinear automorphisms and Weil restriction

Let
Z
be a finite étale scheme over
k
. The
k
-algebra
R=\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{O}_{Z})
is the product for
z\in Z
of finite extensions
k_{z}
of
k
. An
\mathcal{O}_{Z}
-module
V
on
Z
is determined by its fibers, thus it is equivalent to the datum for each
z\in Z
of a
k_{z}
-vector space
V_{z}
; in particular it is always locally free. We say that a
k
-linear endomorphism
f
of
V
is
semilinear
if there is an automorphism
\sigma
of the
k
-algebra
R
such that
f(\lambda x)=\sigma(\lambda)f(x)
for any
x\in V
and
\lambda\in R
. In general
\sigma
is not unique. Nonetheless when
f
is bijective there is a natural choice for
\sigma
: on the support of
V
it is uniquely determined by
f
and outside it can be taken to be the identity. When
V
is a vector bundle on
Z
, semilinear automorphisms can be seen as
k
-points of the algebraic group
\GL^{\textup{s}}_{Z/k}(V)
over
k
constructed as follows. First we have an inclusion

\Res_{Z/k}\GL(V)\subset\GL(V_{0})

where
V_{0}
is
V
seen merely as
k
-vector space. Then
\GL^{\textup{s}}_{Z/k}(V)
is defined as the normalizer of
\Res_{Z/k}\GL(V)
in
\GL(V_{0})
. We have an exact sequence

(4.1)

1\longrightarrow\Res_{Z/k}\GL(V)\longrightarrow\GL^{\textup{s}}_{Z/k}(V)\longrightarrow\Aut_{k}(Z)

of algebraic groups over
k
. Conjugation by a semilinear automorphism
f
of
V
induces an automorphism of
\GL(V)
as a
k
-scheme such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GL(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GL(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\mapsto fgf^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec\sigma}

where
\sigma
is the associated automorphism of
R
. We say that
f
normalizes a closed subscheme
X\subset\GL(V)
if
fXf^{-1}=X
.
The datum of a
Z
-scheme
X
is equivalent to the datum of a
k_{z}
-scheme
X_{z}
for each
z\in Z
; in particular all
Z
-schemes are flat. In what follows we will adopt freely both points of view. For this reason we can perform on group
Z
-schemes of finite type the usual operations available on algebraic groups (quotients, normalizers, etc.). Moreover we have the following relation between Weil restrictions

\Res_{Z/k}X=\prod_{z\in Z}\Res_{k_{z}/k}X_{z}

as soon as they exists (for example if
X
is affine). We profit of the setup to prove the following result that will be repeatedly used in this section:

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
V
be a vector bundle over
Z
,
G\subset\GL(V)
a subgroup
Z
-scheme and
N
its normalizer. Then
\Res_{Z/k}N\subset\GL^{\textup{s}}_{Z/k}(V)
is of finite index in its normalizer.

Proof.

For a vector bundle
E
on
Z
write
E_{0}
when seen as a
k
-vector space. With this notation for a smooth group
Z
-scheme
H
we have
\Lie\Res_{Z/k}H=(\Lie H)_{0}
. In order to prove the statement it suffices to show that
\Res_{Z/k}N
and its normalizer
N^{\prime}
in
\GL^{\textup{s}}_{Z/k}(V)
have same Lie algebra. For, we have the following chain of identities:

\displaystyle\Lie\Res_{Z/k}N

\displaystyle=(\Lie N)_{0}

\displaystyle=\{X\in\Lie\GL(V)\mid[X,\Lie G]\subset\Lie G\}_{0}

\displaystyle=\{X\in\Lie\GL(V)_{0}\mid[X,(\Lie G)_{0}]\subset(\Lie G)_{0}\}

\displaystyle=\{X\in\Lie\GL(V)_{0}\mid[X,\Lie\Res_{Z/k}G]\subset\Lie\Res_{Z/k}G\}

\displaystyle=\Lie N^{\prime}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

We say that a group
Z
-scheme
G
is
reductive
if
G_{z}
is a reductive affine algebraic group over
k_{z}
for each
z\in Z
. Note that this differs from the notion in
[
SGA 3
]
where reductive group schemes are assumed to have connected fibers. Let
G
be a reductive group
Z
-scheme. We write
G^{\circ}
for the group subscheme of
G
whose fiber at
z
is
G_{z}^{\circ}
. We also define

\mathcal{B}(G,Z):=\mathcal{B}(\Res_{Z/k}G,k)=\prod_{z\in Z}\mathcal{B}(\Res_{k_{z}/k}G_{z},k)=\prod_{z\in Z}\mathcal{B}(G_{z},k_{z}),

where the equalities come from
example

3.4
. For a point
x=(x_{z})_{z\in Z}\in\mathcal{B}(G,Z)
we write
\Stab_{G}(x)
for the
Z
-scheme whose fiber at
z
is
\Stab_{G_{z}}(x_{z})
. This formalism will be applied in two contexts below.

4.2.
Étale data

Take
k=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
Z
be a finite étale scheme over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\Gamma
a topological group acting continuously on
Z
via
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-scheme isomorphisms. By a
semilinear representation
of
\Gamma
on
Z
we mean a couple
(V,\rho)
where
V
is a vector bundle on
Z
and
\rho\colon\Gamma\to\GL^{\textup{s}}_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(V)
is a continuous group morphism such that the following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GL_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\textup{s}}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Aut(Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

Geometrically the commutativity of the above diagram means that the action of
\Gamma
on
V
is equivariant with respect the action on
Z
. A closed subscheme
X\subset\GL(V)
is
normalized
by
\Gamma
if
\rho(\gamma)
normalizes
X
for each
\gamma\in\Gamma
.

Definition 4.2
.

A
\Gamma
-étale datum on
Z
is a triple
D=(V,G,\rho)
where

•

(V,\rho)
is a semilinear representation of
\Gamma
on
Z
,

•

G\subset\GL(V)
is a reductive group
Z
-scheme normalized by
\Gamma
.

If the scheme
Z
is clear from the context we will call
D
a
\Gamma
-étale datum. The
\Gamma
-étale datum
D
is
semisimple
if the Zariski closure of
\im(\rho\colon\Gamma\to\GL(V_{0}))
is a reductive subgroup, where
V_{0}
stands for
V
as a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector space.

By a
filtered
resp.
graded
\Gamma
-étale datum
we mean a
\Gamma
-étale datum
D=(V,G,\rho)
together with a filtration resp. a grading where

•

a
filtration
is a
\Gamma
-fixed point
x\in\mathcal{B}(G,Z)
;

•

a
grading
is a structure of graded vector space
V_{z}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}}V_{z}^{(\alpha)}
on
V_{z}
for each
z\in Z
such that
G_{z}\subset\prod_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}}\GL(V_{z}^{(\alpha)})
and
\Gamma
preserves the subspace

\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}V_{\gamma z}^{(\alpha)}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{for each}\hskip 10.00002pt\alpha\;\in\;\mathbb{Q}.

We denote such a graded
\Gamma
-étale datum by
(V^{(\bullet)},G,\rho)
.

An
isomorphism
between
\Gamma
-étale data
(V,G,\rho)
and
(V^{\prime},G^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
is a
\Gamma
-equivariant isomorphism of vector bundles

f\colon\hskip 10.00002ptV\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\;V^{\prime}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{with}\hskip 10.00002ptG^{\prime}=fGf^{-1}.

By an isomorphism of filtered
\Gamma
-étale data we mean an isomorphism of
\Gamma
-étale data which preserves the corresponding points in the building. An isomorphism between graded
\Gamma
-étale data
(V^{(\bullet)},G,\rho)
and
(V^{\prime(\bullet)},G^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
is an isomorphism between the underlying
\Gamma
-étale data that preserves the grading in the sense that
f(V_{z}^{(\alpha)})=V^{\prime(\alpha)}_{z}
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}
and all
z\in Z
.
The
pull-back
of
\Gamma
-étale data along a
\Gamma
-equivariant finite étale morphism
Z^{\prime}\to Z
is defined in the obvious way.

Example 4.3
.

Let
A
be an abelian variety over a field
K
of characteristic
0
. Given an integer
r\geqslant 1
consider the
p
-adic sheaf

E:=[r]_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{p,A}

on
A
where
[r]
is the multiplication by
r
on
A
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p,A}
the trivial
p
-adic sheaf on
A
. To decompose
E
let
\bar{K}
be an algebraic closure of
K
and
A[r](\bar{K})
the
r
-torsion subgroup of
A(\bar{K})
. We identify
A[r](\bar{K})
with a constant group scheme over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and consider its Cartier dual

Z:=A[r](\bar{K})^{\ast}=\Hom(A[r](\bar{K}),\mathbb{G}_{m}).

The Galois group
\Gamma:=\Gal(\bar{K}/K)
acts naturally on
Z
via its action on
A[r](\bar{K})
. The points of
Z
with values in a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra
B
are morphisms
A[r](\bar{K})\to B^{\times}
of (abstract) groups. For
z\in Z
let
k_{z}
be the residue field at
z
and

\chi_{z}\colon A[r](\bar{K})\longrightarrow k_{z}^{\times}

the corresponding character. By precomposing
\chi_{z}
with the canonical projection

\pi_{1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(A_{\bar{K}},0)=\projlim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}A[n](\bar{K})\longrightarrow A[r](\bar{K})

we obtain a character of geometric étale fundamental group of
A_{\bar{K}}
hence a
p
-adic rank
1
local system on
A_{\bar{K}}
. Then

E_{\rvert A_{\bar{K}}}=\bigoplus_{z\in Z}L_{z}.

Let
f\colon X\to A
be a morphism of varieties over
K
with
X
smooth of dimension
d
. Then the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector space

V:=\textup{H}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}^{d}(X_{\bar{K}},f^{\ast}E)=\bigoplus_{z\in Z}\textup{H}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}^{d}(X_{\bar{K}},f^{\ast}L_{z})

is finite dimensional, inherits a natural structure of vector bundle over
Z
and the natural
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-linear action of
\Gamma
on
V
is semilinear. For each
z\in Z
set

G_{z}=\begin{cases}\GL(V_{z})&\textup{if $X$ is not symmetric up to translation,}\\
\GO(V_{z},\theta_{z})&\textup{if $X$ is symmetric up to translation and $d$ is even,}\\
\GSp(V_{z},\theta_{z})&\textup{if $X$ is symmetric up to translation and $d$ is odd}.\end{cases}

Here
symmetric up to a translation
means that there is an automorphism
\iota
of
X
and a point
a\in A(K)
such that
f(\iota(x))=a-f(x)
for all
x\in X
. Poincaré duality then induces a perfect pairing
\theta_{z}\colon V_{z}\otimes V_{z}\to L_{z,a}
. Since the action of
\Gamma
preserves the Poincaré pairing we conclude that

D=(V,G,\rho)

is a
\Gamma
-étale datum over
Z
, where
\rho
is the representation defining the action of
\Gamma
.

Definition 4.4
.

For any filtered
\Gamma
-étale datum
D=(V,G,\rho,x)
, we define the associated graded

\gr D\;=\;(\bar{V}^{(\bullet)},\bar{G},\bar{\rho})

as follows: For each
z\in Z
, let
V_{z}^{\bullet}
be the filtration induced by
x_{z}
. Let
Q_{z}\subset G_{z}
be the stabilizer of
x_{z}
, and consider

\bar{G}_{z}:=Q_{z}/\rad Q_{z}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha}\GL(\bar{V}_{z}^{(\alpha)})\hskip 10.00002pt\text{where}\hskip 10.00002pt\bar{V}_{z}^{(\alpha)}=\gr^{\alpha}V_{z}.

Since
x\in\mathcal{B}(G,Z)
is fixed under
\Gamma
, the subspace
\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}V_{\gamma z}^{\alpha}
is stable under
\Gamma
for each
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}
. Thus
\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}V_{\gamma z}^{(\alpha)}
inherits a natural linear action of
\Gamma
. The induced representation
\bar{\rho}\colon\Gamma\to\GL^{\textup{s}}_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\bar{V})

is semilinear, where
\bar{V}=\bigoplus_{z\in Z}\gr V_{z}
.

4.3.
Finiteness

Let
K
be a number field and
\bar{K}
an algebraic closure of
K
. Here we consider étale data in the above sense with
\Gamma=\Gal(\bar{K}/K)
. For a place
v
of
K
let
\mathbb{F}_{v}
the residue field at
v
. Fix a finite subset
\Sigma
of places of
K
.

Definition 4.5
.

Let
V_{0}
be a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector space and
w\in\mathbb{Z}
. A continuous representation
\rho\colon\Gamma\to\GL(V_{0})
is said to be
pure of weight
w
(with respect to
\Sigma
) if it is unramified outside
\Sigma\cup\{p\}
and for each place
v\not\in\Sigma\cup\{p\}
the characteristic polynomial of any Frobenius element at
v
has integral coefficients and roots of absolute value
|\mathbb{F}_{v}|^{-w/2}
. A
\Gamma
-étale datum
(V,G,\rho)
is
pure of weight
w
if so is the representation
\Gamma\to\GL(V_{0})
where
V_{0}
is
V
seen a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector space.

Let
W
be a vector bundle on
Z
and
H\subset\GL(W)
a reductive group
Z
-scheme. The first result of this section is the following form of Faltings’ finiteness of pure semisimple Galois representations:

Proposition 4.6
.

For
w\in\mathbb{Z}
, there are up to isomorphism only finitely many
\Gamma
-étale data that are semisimple, pure of weight
w
and such that
(G,V)\cong(H,W)
.

Proof.

We correct some inaccuracies in
[
LV20
, lemma 2.6]
, where the reduction to
[
Ric67
, th. 7.1]
is wrong, and in
[
LS20
, lemma 5.49]
where
[
Ser94
, th. 4.4.5]
is applied to a number field instead of a local field. We may assume
(G,V)=(H,W)
. By
[
Del85
, th. 3.1]
up to isomorphism there are only finitely many continuous representations
\Gamma\to\GL(W_{0})
that are semisimple and pure of weight
w
with respect to
\Sigma
, where
W_{0}
stands for
W
seen as
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector space. Let
\rho
be such a representation. Let
N
be the normalizer of the subgroup
H\subset\GL(W)
. It suffices to check that there are only finitely many
N(Z)
-conjugacy classes in the isomorphism class of
\rho
. The image of
\rho
is contained in the normalizer
N^{\prime}
of
\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}H\subset\GL(W_{0})
. Let
L\subset N^{\prime}
be the Zariski closure of the image of
\rho
. It suffices to show that

\mathcal{C}\;:=\;\{L^{\prime}\subset N^{\prime}\mid L^{\prime}=gLg^{-1},g\in\GL(W_{0})(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\}

is a finite union of
N(Z)
-orbits. Now the subgroup
\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}N\subset N^{\prime}
is of finite index by
lemma

4.1
below. Therefore it suffices to prove that
\mathcal{C}
is a finite union of
N^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-orbits. The representation
\rho
is semisimple by hypothesis, thus the connected component of
L
is reductive. According to
proposition

A.1
, the set

\{L^{\prime}\subset(N^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}{\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})\mid L^{\prime}=gLg^{-1},g\in\GL(W_{0})(\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\}

is a finite union of
N^{\prime}(\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-orbits, where
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is an algebraic closure of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. We then conclude by
[
Ser94
, th. 4.4.5]
.
∎

Corollary 4.7
.

Let
w\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathcal{I}\subset\mathcal{B}(H,Z)/H^{\circ}(Z)
a finite subset. Then up to isomorphism there are only finitely many graded
\Gamma
-étale data on
Z
arising as the graded of a filtered étale data
D=(V,G,\rho,x)
such that

•

D
is pure of weight
w
,

•

\gr D
is semisimple, and

•

there is an isomorphism
(V,G)\cong(W,H)
sending
x
in
\mathcal{I}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show the statement when
\mathcal{I}=\{[x]\}
for some
x\in\mathcal{B}(H,Z)
. In this case, up to isomorphism we may write any filtered
\Gamma
-étale datum in question as a quadruple
(W,H,\rho,x)
where now only
\rho
is variable. Let
Q
be the stabilizer of
x
and
W^{\bullet}
the filtration induced on
W
by
x
. We conclude by applying
proposition

4.6
with
Q^{\textup{ss}}
and
\gr W^{\bullet}
.
∎

In order to apply
corollary

4.7
it will be important to have a finite subset
\mathcal{I}
permitting to construct the semisimplification of all representations in question:

Lemma 4.8
.

Suppose that the subgroup
H\subset\GL(W)
is of finite index in its normalizer. Then there exists a finite subset

\mathcal{I}\subset\mathcal{B}(H,Z)/H(Z)

with the following property: for each semilinear representation
\rho\colon\Gamma\to\GL_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\textup{s}}(W)
normalizing
H
there is
x\in\mathcal{B}(H,Z)
fixed under
\Gamma
with image in
\mathcal{I}
such that the graded of the filtered
\Gamma
-étale datum
(W,H,\rho,x)
is semisimple.

Proof.

Let
N\subset\GL(W)
be the normalizer of
H
and
N^{\prime}\subset\GL_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\textup{s}}(W)
the normalizer of
\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}H
. Since
H\subset N
is of finite index the subgroup
\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}N\subset N^{\prime}
is of finite index by
lemma

4.1
. Therefore, we have the following chains of identities:

\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}H^{\circ}=\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}N^{\circ}=N^{\prime\circ},\hskip 20.00003pt\mathcal{B}(H,Z)=\mathcal{B}(N,Z)=\mathcal{B}(N^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

On the other hand the set

\Par(H^{\circ})(Z)/H(Z)=\prod_{z\in Z}\Par(H^{\circ}_{z})(k_{z})/H_{z}(k_{z}),

is finite, where
k_{z}
is the residue field at
z
. Let
\mathcal{P}\subset\Par(H^{\circ})(Z)
be a finite set of representatives of the preceding quotient. According to
[
BMR20
, lemma 2.1]
for each parabolic subgroup
P\in\mathcal{P}
there is
x_{P}\in\mathcal{B}(H,Z)
such that
\Stab_{N^{\prime}}(x_{P})
is the normalizer of
\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}P
in
N^{\prime}
. We claim that the set

\mathcal{I}=\{[x_{P}]\mid P\in\mathcal{P}\}\subset\mathcal{B}(H,Z)/H(Z)

does the job. Indeed let
\rho\colon\Gamma\to\GL_{Z/k}^{\textup{s}}(W)
be a semilinear representation normalizing
H
. By
[
BMR20
, corollary 3.5]
there is a point
y
in
\mathcal{B}(H,Z)=\mathcal{B}(N^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
fixed under
\Gamma
such that the graded of the filtered
\Gamma
-étale datum
(W,H,\rho,y)
is semisimple. Then
\Stab_{H}(y)^{\circ}=hPh^{-1}
for some
P\in\mathcal{P}
and
h\in H(Z)
. By construction the stabilizer of
x:=hx_{P}
is the normalizer of
\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\Stab_{H}(y)^{\circ}
. Since
\Gamma
fixes
y
the image of
\rho
is contained in
\Stab_{N^{\prime}}(x)
hence
\Gamma
fixes
x
too. Moreover

\rad\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\Stab_{H}(y)=\rad\Stab_{N^{\prime}}(x)

thus the graded of filtered
\Gamma
-étale datum
(W,H,\rho,x)
is semisimple by
[
BMR20
, example 4.9]
. This concludes the proof.
∎

4.4.
de Rham data

Let
R=\mathcal{R}[\tfrac{1}{p}]
where
\mathcal{R}
is a finite étale
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra. Equivalently
R
is a finite product of finite unramified extensions of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The ring
\mathcal{R}
coincides with the ring of Witt vectors of its reduction modulo
p
and therefore inherits a Frobenius endomorphism
\sigma
. We denote again by
\sigma
the unique ring endomorphism of
R
extending
\sigma
. Let
V
be a vector bundle on
Z=\Spec R
. In this section we always suppose semilinear endomorphisms
\varphi
of
V
to be
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-linear endomorphism such that
\varphi(\lambda v)=\sigma(\lambda)\varphi(v)
for all
\lambda\in R
and
v\in V
.

Definition 4.9
.

A
de Rham datum on
Z
is a quadruple
D=(V,G,h,\varphi)
where

•

V
is a vector bundle over
Z
,

•

G\subset\GL(V)
is a reductive group
Z
-scheme,

•

h\in\mathcal{B}(G,Z)
,

•

\varphi
is a bijective semilinear endomorphism of
V
normalizing
G
.

If the scheme
Z
is clear from the context we will simply call
D
a de Rham datum. By a
filtered
resp.
graded de Rham datum
we mean a de Rham datum
D=(V,G,h,\varphi)
together with a filtration resp. a grading, where

•

a
filtration
is a point
x\in\mathcal{B}(G,Z)
fixed under
\varphi
;

•

a
grading
is the datum for each
z\in Z
of a structure of graded
k_{z}
-vector space
V_{z}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}}V_{z}^{(\alpha)}
such that the graded pieces are stable under
\varphi_{z}
and such that

G_{z}\subset\prod_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}}\GL(V_{z}^{(\alpha)}).

An
isomorphism
between de Rham data
(V,G,h,\varphi)
and
(V^{\prime},G^{\prime},h^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
is an isomorphism of vector bundles

f\colon\hskip 10.00002ptV\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\;V^{\prime}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{with}\hskip 10.00002ptG^{\prime}=fGf^{-1},\hskip 10.00002pt\varphi^{\prime}=f\varphi f^{-1},\hskip 10.00002pth^{\prime}=F_{\ast}h,

where
F\colon G\to G^{\prime}
is the conjugation by
f
. By an isomorphism of filtered de Rham data we mean an isomorphism of de Rham data which preserves the given points in the building. An isomorphism between graded de Rham data
(V^{(\bullet)},G,h,\varphi)
and
(V^{\prime(\bullet)},G^{\prime},h^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
is an isomorphism between the underlying de Rham data that preserves the grading in the sense that
f(V_{z}^{(\alpha)})=V^{\prime(\alpha)}_{z}
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}
and all
z\in Z
. The
pull-back
of de Rham data along a finite étale morphism
Z^{\prime}\to Z
is defined in the obvious way.

Example 4.10
.

Let
K
be an unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and
\mathcal{A}
an abelian scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Given an integer
r\geqslant 1
nondivisible by
p
consider the vector bundle

\mathcal{E}:=[r]_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}

on
\mathcal{A}
where
[r]
is the multiplication by
r
on
\mathcal{A}
. The morphism
[r]
is finite étale thus the vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
comes equipped with an integrable connection
\nabla
induced by the canonical derivation on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}
. To decompose
\mathcal{E}
let
\mathcal{A}^{\natural}
be the universal vector extension of the dual abelian scheme
\mathcal{A}^{\ast}
. From a modular point of view
\mathcal{A}^{\natural}
parametrizes rank
1
connections on
\mathcal{A}
, that is, couples
(\mathcal{L},\nabla)
made of a line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathcal{A}
and a (necessarily integrable) connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{L}
. Tensor product of line bundles together with a connection endow
\mathcal{A}^{\natural}
with a structure of group scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Consider the universal rank
1
connection
(\mathcal{L},\nabla)
on
\mathcal{A}^{\natural}\times\mathcal{A}
. The closed subscheme of
r
-torsion points
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{A}^{\natural}[r]
is finite étale over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Then

(\mathcal{E},\nabla)=\pi_{\ast}(\mathcal{L},\nabla)\hskip 10.00002pt\textup{where $\pi\colon\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}$ is the second projection}.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{A}
be a morphism of finite type
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-schemes with
\mathcal{X}
smooth of relative dimension
d
. Set
\mathcal{Y}:=\mathcal{Z}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{X}
and consider the
d
-th relative de Rham cohomology group

\mathcal{V}:=\mathcal{H}_{\textup{dR}}^{d}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{O}_{K};f^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},\nabla))=\mathcal{H}^{d}_{\textup{dR}}(\mathcal{Y}/\mathcal{Z};(f\times\id)^{\ast}(\mathcal{L},\nabla)).

Suppose that
\mathcal{V}
is locally free as well as the graded pieces of the Hodge filtration on
\mathcal{V}
. Let
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}
be the pull-back of
\mathcal{X}
along the morphism
[r]
. The crystalline-de Rham comparison theorem furnishes a canonical isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-modules

\mathcal{V}\cong\textup{H}^{d}_{\textup{crys}}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}}_{p}/\mathcal{O}_{K})

where the subscript
p
stands for the reduction modulo
p
. By functoriality of crystalline cohomology
\mathcal{V}
inherits a Frobenius operator
\varphi
. Let
A
,
X
,
Y
and
Z
be the generic fiber respectively of
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{X}
,
\mathcal{Y}
and
\mathcal{Z}
. Then

V:=\mathcal{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}K=\mathcal{H}^{d}_{\textup{dR}}(Y/Z;\mathcal{L},\nabla)

is a vector bundle over
Z
and as
K
-vector spaces we have

V_{0}=\textup{H}^{d}_{\textup{dR}}(X/K;\mathcal{E},\nabla).

The Frobenius operator
\varphi
extends to a semilinear automorphism of
V
denoted
\varphi
again. For each
z\in Z
set

G_{z}=\begin{cases}\GL(V_{z})&\textup{if $X$ is not symmetric up to translation,}\\
\GO(V_{z},\theta_{z})&\textup{if $X$ is symmetric up to translation and $d$ is even,}\\
\GSp(V_{z},\theta_{z})&\textup{if $X$ is symmetric up to translation and $d$ is odd}.\end{cases}

As in
example

4.3

symmetric up to a translation
means that there is an automorphism
\iota
of
X
and a point
a\in A(K)
such that
f(\iota(x))=a-f(x)
for all
x\in X
. Poincaré duality then induces a perfect pairing
\theta_{z}\colon V_{z}\otimes V_{z}\to L_{z,a}
. The Frobenius operator
\varphi
normalizes
G
because the crystalline-de Rham comparison isomorphism is compatible with the Poincaré pairing and the Frobenius operator preserves it up to a scalar multiple. The vector bundle
V
comes with the Hodge filtration

F^{\bullet}\;:\hskip 10.00002ptV\;=\;F^{0}\;\supset\;F^{1}\;\supset\;\cdots\;\supset\;F^{d}\;\supset\;F^{d+1}\;=\;0,

where
F^{i}=\im(\textup{R}^{d}\pi_{Z\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet\geqslant i}_{Y/Z}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\pi_{A}^{\ast}\mathcal{L})\to V)
for the projection
\pi_{Z}\colon Y\to Z
.
Moreover, if
X
is symmetric up to a translation, then the Hodge filtration is autodual with respect to the Poincaré pairing in the sense that
(F^{i})^{\perp}=F^{d+1-i}
for each
i
(this can be checked on fibers at geometric points of
Z
, so it follows from the statement over the complex numbers in
corollary

B.4
).
Thus the Hodge filtration
F^{\bullet}
defines a point
h\in\mathcal{B}(G,Z)
, in other words the quadruple

D=(V,G,h,\varphi)

is a de Rham datum on
Z
.

Definition 4.11
.

The associated graded
\gr D=(\bar{V},\bar{G},\bar{h},\bar{\varphi},\bar{V}^{(\bullet)})
of a filtered de Rham datum
D=(V,G,h,\varphi,x)
is defined as follows. For each point
z\in Z
let
V_{z}^{\bullet}
be the filtration which is induced by
x_{z}
, and let
Q_{z}\subset G_{z}
be the parabolic subgroup stabilizing
x_{z}
. Then we put

\bar{G}_{z}:=Q_{z}/\rad Q_{z}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\prod_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}}\GL(V^{(\alpha)}_{z})

where
V^{(\alpha)}_{z}:=\gr^{\alpha}V_{z}
, and we define
\bar{h}_{z}:=\pr_{Q_{z}}(h_{z})
and
\bar{\varphi}_{z}:=\gr\varphi_{z}
.

4.5.
From graded to filtered data

Let
D_{i}=(V_{i},G_{i},h_{i},\varphi_{i},x_{i})
for
i=1,2
be two filtered de Rham data. We want to understand the relation between them if the associated graded data are isomorphic. As a reference datum we fix
(V,G,\varphi)

where

•

V
is a vector bundle on
Z
,

•

G\subset\GL(V)
is a reductive subgroup scheme over
Z
,

•

\varphi\colon V\to V
is a semilinear endomorphism normalizing
G
.

Suppose that for
i=1,2
we are given isomorphisms of vector bundles

\tau_{i}\colon\hskip 10.00002ptV_{i}\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\;V\hskip 10.00002pt\text{such that}\hskip 10.00002ptG\;=\;\tau_{i}G_{i}\tau_{i}^{-1}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\varphi\;=\;\tau_{i}\varphi_{i}\tau_{i}^{-1}.

Via these isomorphisms we will identify the points
x_{i},h_{i}\in\mathcal{B}(G_{i},k)
with points in
\mathcal{B}(G,Z)
which we again denote
x_{i},h_{i}
. Let
Q_{i}\subset G
denote the stabilizer of
x_{i}
, with Levi quotient
Q_{i}^{\textup{ss}}=Q_{i}/\rad Q_{i}
. With notation as in
section

3.4
consider the projection

\pr_{i}\colon\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{B}(G,Z)\;=\;\prod_{z\in Z}\mathcal{B}(G_{z},k_{z})\;\longrightarrow\;\mathcal{B}(Q_{i}^{\textup{ss}},Z)\;=\;\prod_{z\in Z}\mathcal{B}(Q_{i,z}^{\textup{ss}},k_{z}).

In general an isomorphism between graded cannot be lifted to an isomorphism of the original filtered one. Nonetheless the filtered data satisfy a weaker relation (to be compared with
definition

6.2
):

Proposition 4.12
.

If in the above setup the graded de Rham data
\gr D_{1}
and
\gr D_{2}
are isomorphic and for every
z\in Z
the subgroup
G_{z}\subset\GL(V_{z})
is full in the sense of
definition

3.17
, then there exists
g\in G(Z)
such that

\displaystyle x_{2}=gx_{1},

\displaystyle g\varphi g^{-1}\varphi^{-1}\in\rad Q_{2},

\displaystyle\pr_{2}(h_{2})=\pr_{2}(gh_{1}).

Proof.

By reasoning pointwise we may assume
Z
is a singleton. Then
Z=\Spec k
for a finite unramified extension
k
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Via the isomorphisms
f_{i}
we make the identifications

V_{i}=V,\hskip 10.00002ptG_{i}=G\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\varphi_{i}=\varphi\hskip 10.00002pt\text{for}\hskip 10.00002pti=1,2.

Let
V_{i}^{\alpha}\subset V
be the steps of the filtration induced by
x_{i}
and let
V_{i}^{(\alpha)}=V_{i}^{\alpha}/V_{i}^{<\alpha}
be the associated graded pieces, for each
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}
. By assumption there exists an isomorphism
\gr D_{1}\cong\gr D_{2}
of graded de Rham data, which by definition consists of a collection of
K
-linear isomorphisms
\bar{g}^{(\alpha)}\colon V_{1}^{(\alpha)}\to V_{2}^{(\alpha)}
whose direct sum
\bar{g}
has the following properties:

(1)

Q_{2}^{\textup{ss}}=\bar{g}Q_{1}^{\textup{ss}}\bar{g}^{-1}
,

(2)

\bar{\varphi}=\bar{g}\bar{\varphi}\bar{g}^{-1}
,

(3)

\pr_{2}(h_{2})=\bar{g}_{\ast}\pr_{1}(h_{1})
.

We now use the assumption that the subgroup
G\subset\GL(V)
is full.
Note that the filtrations on
V
induced by the two points
x_{1},x_{2}
are conjugate under
\GL(V)(k)
since their filtered pieces have the same dimension
\dim V_{1}^{\alpha}=\dim V_{2}^{\alpha}
. Since the corresponding weights
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}
also match, it follows that the two points
x_{1},x_{2}
are conjugate under
\GL(V)(k)
.
By the definition of full subgroups we then have a finite subset
I\subset\mathbb{Q}
such that

\varepsilon_{x_{i},I}\colon\hskip 10.00002ptQ_{i}^{\textup{ss}}\;\longrightarrow\;\prod_{\alpha\in I}\GL(V_{i}^{(\alpha)})

is an embedding and
\Tran_{G}(x_{1},x_{2})\to\gr_{I}\Tran_{\GL(V)}(x_{1},x_{2})
is surjective. It follows that the map on
k
-points

\Tran_{G}(x_{1},x_{2})(k)\longrightarrow\gr_{I}\Tran_{\GL(V)}(x_{1},x_{2})(k)

is also surjective by the remark after
lemma

3.16
. Since
\bar{g}\in\gr_{I}\Tran_{\GL(V)}(x_{1},x_{2})(k)
by (1), this gives an element
g\in\Tran_{G}(x_{1},x_{2})(k)\subset G(k)
lifting
\bar{g}
. By definition of the transporter
\Tran_{G}(x_{1},x_{2})
we have
gx_{1}=x_{2}
. To prove
g\varphi g^{-1}\varphi^{-1}\in\rad Q_{2}

note that
g\varphi g^{-1}\varphi^{-1}
belongs to
Q_{2}
because
\varphi
fixes
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
. The wanted assertion then follows from condition (2) and the injectivity of
\varepsilon_{x_{2},I}
. Finally from (3) we get
\pr_{2}(h_{2})=\pr_{2}(gh_{1})
which concludes the proof.
∎

5.
p
-adic Hodge theory

We now discuss how to pass from étale to de Rham data via
p
-adic Hodge theory, and how global purity on the étale side leads to positivity in the sense of
section

3.5
on the de Rham side.

5.1.
Crystalline representations

Let
K
be a finite unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{K}
,
\bar{K}
an algebraic closure of
K
and
\Gamma=\Gal(\bar{K}/K)
. The ring of crystalline periods

B:=B_{\textup{crys}}

is an integral domain with an algebra structure over the maximal unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. It comes equipped with an action of
\Gamma
, a Frobenius operator
\varphi
and a (non-increasing, exhausting, separated) filtration
h^{\bullet}B=(h^{\alpha}B)_{\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}}
stable under
\Gamma
. For the construction of
B
and the properties cited below see for example
[
FO
]
or
[
BC09
, II.9]
.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
V
be a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector space endowed with a linear action of
\Gamma
. When
V
is finite dimensional, we say that the representation
V
is
crystalline
if it is continuous and
\dim_{K}(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B)^{\Gamma}=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}V
. When
V
is infinite dimensional, we say that
V
is
crystalline
if it is an increasing union of finite dimensional crystalline subrepresentations.

We then set

V_{\textup{dR}}:=(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B)^{\Gamma},\hskip 20.00003pth^{\alpha}V:=(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}h^{\alpha}B)^{\Gamma},

so we have a natural isomorphism

V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B\cong V_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{K}B
. The filtration
h_{V}:=h^{\bullet}V
is non-increasing, exhausting and separated; it is called the
Hodge filtration
of
V_{\textup{dR}}
. The operator
\varphi
induces a bijective semilinear endomorphism
\varphi_{V}
of
V_{\textup{dR}}
which we call again the Frobenius operator. The class of crystalline representations is stable under subquotients and tensor products. For a morphism of crystalline representations
f\colon V\to W
the resulting
K
-linear map
f_{\textup{dR}}\colon V_{\textup{dR}}\to W_{\textup{dR}}
is compatible with the induced Frobenius operators on
V_{\textup{dR}}
and
W_{\textup{dR}}
. The functor
V\rightsquigarrow V_{\textup{dR}}
is exact and if
f
is injective resp. surjective then
h_{V}
is the restriction to
V
of
h_{W}
resp.
h_{W}
is the quotient of
h_{V}
. We will refer to this property as
exactness of the Hodge filtration
. For crystalline representations
V
and
W
we have a canonical isomorphism

(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}W)_{\textup{dR}}=V_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{K}W_{\textup{dR}}

compatible with the Frobenius operators and the Hodge filtrations. When
V
is finite dimensional the filtration
h_{V}
can be seen as a point of
\mathcal{B}(\GL(V_{\textup{dR}}),K)
by
example

3.3
.

Example 5.2
.

An unramified representation
\rho\colon\Gamma\to\GL(V)
is crystalline; see for instance
[
FO
, prop. 7.17]
. When the image of
\rho
is finite this can be seen by the following argument. In this case
\Ker\rho=\Gamma^{\prime}:=\Gal(\bar{K}/K^{\prime})
for some finite unramified extension
K^{\prime}
of
K
and

\displaystyle(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B)^{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=[(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B)^{\Gamma^{\prime}}]^{\Gamma/\Gamma^{\prime}}=(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B^{\Gamma^{\prime}})^{\Gal(K^{\prime}/K)}=(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}K^{\prime})^{\Gal(K^{\prime}/K)}.

So by Galois descent
\dim_{K}(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B)^{\Gamma}=\dim_{K}(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}K^{\prime})^{\Gal(K^{\prime}/K)}=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}V
.

Let
A
be a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra endowed with a crystalline action of
\Gamma
compatible with the ring structure of
A
, that is
\gamma(ab)=(\gamma a)(\gamma b)
for all
a,b\in A
and
\gamma\in\Gamma
. A
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-linear action of
\Gamma
on an
A
-module
M
is
semilinear
if for all
\gamma\in\Gamma
,
a\in A
and
m\in M
we have
\gamma(am)=(\gamma a)(\gamma m)
. Given an
A
-module
M
with a crystalline semilinear action of
\Gamma
the
K
-vector space
M_{\textup{dR}}
inherits a natural structure of
A_{\textup{dR}}
-module. For a
\Gamma
-equivariant morphism of
A
-modules
f\colon M\to N
the induced map
f_{\textup{dR}}\colon M_{\textup{dR}}\to N_{\textup{dR}}
is a morphism of
A_{\textup{dR}}
-modules.

Lemma 5.3
.

The so-defined functor

\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{$A$-modules with a crystalline}\\
\textup{semilinear action of $\Gamma$}\end{array}\right\}\;\longrightarrow\;\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{$A_{\textup{dR}}$-modules}\end{array}\right\}

is exact, symmetric monoidal and compatible with pull-back along
\Gamma
-equivariant morphisms of affine
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-schemes with a crystalline action of
\Gamma
. An
A
-module
M
with a crystalline semilinear action of
\Gamma
is finitely generated if and only if
M_{\textup{dR}}
is.

Proof.

The exactness comes from that of the functor
V\mapsto V_{\textup{dR}}
. Given
A
-modules
M
and
N
with a crystalline semilinear action of
\Gamma
, we have

\displaystyle(M\otimes_{A}N)_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{K}B

\displaystyle\cong(M\otimes_{A}N)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B

\displaystyle=(M\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B)\otimes_{A\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B}(N\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B)

\displaystyle\cong(M_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{K}B)\otimes_{A_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{K}B}(N_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{K}B)

\displaystyle=(M_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{A_{\textup{dR}}}N_{\textup{dR}})\otimes_{K}B

Passing to
\Gamma
-invariants we obtain a natural isomorphism

\alpha_{M,N}\colon(M\otimes_{A}N)_{\textup{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}M_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{A_{\textup{dR}}}N_{\textup{dR}}.

This also show that the functor
M\mapsto M_{\textup{dR}}
is compatible with pull-back. It remains to prove that the functor is symmetric. For, notice that the commutativity of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{A_{\textup{dR}}}N_{\textup{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(M\otimes_{A}N)_{\textup{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{A_{\textup{dR}}}M_{\textup{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(N\otimes_{A}M)_{\textup{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{M,N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x\otimes y\mapsto y\otimes x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x\otimes y\mapsto y\otimes x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{N,M}}

can be tested after extending scalars to
B
where it becomes clear. The last statement follows from the fact that finite generation can be tested after extending scalars to
B
; see
[
Stacks
,
Theorem 08XD
]
.
∎

Let
X
be an affine
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-scheme endowed with an action of
\Gamma
defined by a group morphism
\sigma\colon\Gamma\to\Aut(X)
. Then

\Gamma\times\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\longrightarrow\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}),\hskip 10.00002pt(\gamma,f)\longmapsto\sigma(\gamma^{-1})^{\ast}f

defines a linear action of
\Gamma
on
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
compatible with the ring structure.

Definition 5.4
.

We say that the action of
\Gamma
on
X
is
crystalline
if
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
is a crystalline representation. If the action of
\Gamma
on
X
is crystalline, then the
K
-scheme

X_{\textup{dR}}:=\Spec\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})_{\textup{dR}}

comes with a natural isomorphism
X\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B\cong X_{\textup{dR}}\times_{K}B

of
B
-schemes. The Frobenius operator on
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})_{\textup{dR}}
induces an automorphism
\varphi_{X}
of
X_{\textup{dR}}
as a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-scheme. We write
h_{X}
for the Hodge filtration on
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})_{\textup{dR}}
.

The construction is functorial: For a
\Gamma
-equivariant morphism
f\colon X\to Y
between affine
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-schemes endowed with a crystalline action of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
let
f_{\textup{dR}}\colon X_{\textup{dR}}\to Y_{\textup{dR}}
be the induced morphism.

Lemma 5.5
.

The so-defined functor

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{affine $\mathbb{Q}_{p}$-schemes}\\
\textup{with a crystalline action of $\Gamma$}\end{array}\right\}}&\longrightarrow&{\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{affine $K$-schemes}\end{array}\right\}}\\
X&\longmapsto&X_{\textup{dR}}\end{array}

is compatible with fiber products. Moreover, let
f\colon X\to Y
be a
\Gamma
-equivariant morphism between affine
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-schemes endowed with a crystalline action of
\Gamma
. Then,

(1)

given a property
\mathsf{P}
of schemes morphisms that can be checked after faithfully flat base change, the morphism
f
has the property
\mathsf{P}
if and only if
f_{\textup{dR}}
does;

(2)

the following square is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\textup{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\textup{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\textup{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\textup{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\textup{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\textup{dR}}}

Proof.

Compatibility with tensor product comes from
lemma

5.3
while (2) is just the functoriality of the Frobenius endomorphism. Statement (1) is clear because the injections
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\hookrightarrow B
and
K\hookrightarrow B
are faithfully flat ring morphisms.
∎

Example 5.6
.

Let
X
be a finite étale
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-scheme with an unramified action of
\Gamma
. Since
\Aut(X)
is finite, the representation
\rho\colon\Gamma\to\GL(\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}))
has finite image. As in
example

5.2
write
\Ker\rho=\Gal(\bar{K}/K^{\prime})
for some finite unramified extension
K^{\prime}
of
K
. Then

X_{\textup{dR}}=X_{K^{\prime}}/\Gal(K^{\prime}/K)

where
\Gal(K^{\prime}/K)
acts on
X_{K^{\prime}}=\Spec\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}K^{\prime}
via its diagonal action on
both

\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
and
K^{\prime}
. Moreover, we have the set-theoretical identity

X_{\textup{dR}}=X(\bar{K})/\Gamma

where
\Gamma
acts on
X(\bar{K})
via its diagonal action on both
X
and
\bar{K}
. With this in mind, the degree of a point in
X_{\textup{dR}}
is the length of the corresponding orbit under
\Gamma
.

It will be useful to have a handy condition to prove that the action on an affine scheme is crystalline.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
X=\Spec A
be an affine
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-scheme with a crystalline action of
\Gamma
and let
Y
be an affine
X
-scheme of finite type with an action of
\Gamma
such that the structural morphism
Y\to X
is
\Gamma
-equivariant. Then, the action on
Y
is crystalline if and only if there are an
A
-module
M
of finite type with a semilinear crystalline action of
\Gamma
and a
\Gamma
-equivariant closed embedding
Y\hookrightarrow\Spec(\Sym_{A}M)
of
X
-schemes.

Proof.

Suppose that the action of
\Gamma
on
R=\Gamma(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})
is crystalline. Let
V\subset R
be a finite dimensional subrepresentation generating
R
as an
A
-algebra. The
A
-submodule
M\subset R
generated by
V
is stable under
\Gamma
and finitely generated as an
A
-module. Then
M
does the job. Conversely let
M
be an
A
-module with a crystalline semilinear action of
\Gamma
and
f\colon S:=\Sym_{A}M\to R
a
\Gamma
-equivariant surjective morphism of
A
-algebras. For each integer
n\geqslant 0
the representation

S_{n}:=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}\Sym^{i}_{A}M

is crystalline, thus so
R_{n}:=f(S_{n})\subset R
. Since
S=\bigcup_{n=0}^{\infty}S_{n}
and
\varphi
is surjective we have
R=\bigcup_{n=0}^{\infty}R_{n}
which concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
X\to T
and
\pi\colon T\to S
be
\Gamma
-equivariant morphisms between affine varieties over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
endowed with a crystalline action of
\Gamma
. Suppose that
\pi
is finite flat. Then the natural action of
\Gamma
on
\Res_{T/S}X
is crystalline and

(\Res_{T/S}X)_{\textup{dR}}=\Res_{T_{\textup{dR}}/S_{\textup{dR}}}X_{\textup{dR}}.

Proof.

By
lemma

5.7
there is a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{T}
-module
V
with a crystalline semilinear action on
\Gamma(T,V)
and a
\Gamma
-equivariant closed embedding
i\colon X\hookrightarrow\Spec_{T}{\Sym V}
. Then the morphism induced by Weil restriction

j\colon\Res_{T/S}X\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Res_{T/S}(\Spec_{T}{\Sym V})=\Spec_{S}{\Sym\pi_{\ast}T}

is a closed immersion. The group
\Gamma
acts naturally on
\Res_{T/S}X
and the closed immersion
j
is
\Gamma
-equivariant.
Lemma

5.7
then implies that the action of
\Gamma
on
\Res_{T/S}X
is crystalline. Now note that there is a natural morphism of
S_{\textup{dR}}
-schemes

f\colon(\Res_{T/S}X)_{\textup{dR}}\longrightarrow\Res_{T_{\textup{dR}}/S_{\textup{dR}}}X_{\textup{dR}}.

Indeed by universal property of the Weil restriction such a morphism
f
is given by a morphism of
T_{\textup{dR}}
-schemes
(\Res_{T/S}X)_{\textup{dR}}\times_{S_{\textup{dR}}}T_{\textup{dR}}\to X_{\textup{dR}}
still denoted
f
. Similarly the identity of
\Res_{T/S}X
is given by a morphism of
T
-schemes

g\colon\Res_{T/S}(X)\times_{S}T\longrightarrow X.

The morphism
g
is
\Gamma
-equivariant and we set

f:=g_{\textup{dR}}\colon(\Res_{T/S}X)_{\textup{dR}}\times_{S}T_{\textup{dR}}=(\Res_{T/S}(X)\times_{S}T)_{\textup{dR}}\longrightarrow X_{\textup{dR}}

where the identity comes from
lemma

5.5
. In order to conclude the proof it suffices to show that
f
is an isomorphism after extending scalars to
B
. The construction of the Weil restriction is compatible with scalars extension thus

\displaystyle(\Res_{T/S}X)_{\textup{dR}}\times_{K}B

\displaystyle\cong\Res_{T/S}(X)\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B=\Res_{T_{B}/S_{B}}(X_{B}),

\displaystyle(\Res_{T_{\textup{dR}}/S_{\textup{dR}}}X_{\textup{dR}})\times_{K}B

\displaystyle=\Res_{T_{\textup{dR},B}/S_{\textup{dR},B}}(X_{\textup{dR},B})\cong\Res_{T_{B}/S_{B}}(X_{B}).

The endomorphism of
\Res_{T_{B}/S_{B}}(X_{B})
induced by
f
via the preceding isomorphisms is just the identity, which concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.9

(A variation on the construction)
.

Let
V
be a
K
-vector space with a crystalline
K
-linear action of
\Gamma
. Write
V_{0}
for
V
seen simply as a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector space. Then
V_{0,\textup{dR}}=V_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}K
where

V_{\textup{dR}}:=(V\otimes_{K}B)^{\Gamma}.

The construction of
V_{\textup{dR}}
is functorial with respect to
K
-linear
\Gamma
-equivariant maps. This gives rise to a functor

\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{$K$-vector spaces with}\\
\textup{a crystalline $K$-linear action of $\Gamma$}\end{array}\right\}\longrightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{$K$-vector spaces}\end{array}\right\}

which is exact and compatible with tensor product. The Hodge filtration is again exact in the above sense and compatible with tensor product. Similarly, for an affine scheme
X=\Spec A
over
K
with a crystalline action of
\Gamma
by
K
-scheme isomorphisms we define

X_{\textup{dR}}=\Spec{(A\otimes_{K}B)^{\Gamma}}.

This construction is functorial with respect to
\Gamma
-equivariant morphisms of
K
-schemes, hence gives rise to a functor

\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{$K$-schemes with}\\
\textup{a crystalline $K$-linear action of $\Gamma$}\end{array}\right\}\longrightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{$K$-schemes}\end{array}\right\}

which is compatible with fibered products.

5.2.
Affine group schemes with crystalline actions

Let
Z=\Spec R
be an affine
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-scheme endowed with a crystalline action of
\Gamma
and let
V
be a vector bundle on
Z
together with a crystalline semilinear action on the
R
-module
\Gamma(Z,V)
. In this section we write
\mathbb{V}(V)=\Spec\Sym V^{\vee}
for the total space of
V
. Consider a subgroup
Z
-scheme
G\subset\GL(V)
normalized by
\Gamma
. The action by conjugation of
\Gamma
on
G
is crystalline. Indeed the closed embedding

G\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathbb{V}(\End V\oplus\mathcal{O}_{Z}),\hskip 10.00002ptg\longmapsto(g,\det(g)^{-1})

is
\Gamma
-equivariant with respect to the action on
\End(V\oplus\det V)
given by

\gamma.(f,\lambda)=(\rho(\gamma)f\rho(\gamma)^{-1},\lambda),\hskip 20.00003pt\textup{for }\gamma\in\Gamma,f\in\End(V),\textup{ and }\lambda\in\det V,

hence we conclude by
lemma

5.7
. The group law and the inversion on
G
are equivariant morphisms for the action of
\Gamma
and the neutral element of
G
is fixed under
\Gamma
. Thus
G_{\textup{dR}}
is group scheme over
Z_{\textup{dR}}
, and actually a subgroup

G_{\textup{dR}}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\GL(V)_{\textup{dR}}=\GL(V_{\textup{dR}})

because
G\hookrightarrow\GL(V)
is
\Gamma
-equivariant when
\Gamma
acts on
\GL(V)
by conjugation.

Lemma 5.10
.

In the above setup suppose
Z
is finite étale. Then the following hold:

(1)

For any normal subgroup
H\subset G
normalized by
\Gamma
the action by conjugation of
\Gamma
on
G/H
is crystalline and

(G/H)_{\textup{dR}}=G_{\textup{dR}}/H_{\textup{dR}}.

(2)

The unipotent radical
\rad G\subset G
is normalized by
\Gamma
and

(\rad G)_{\textup{dR}}=\rad(G_{\textup{dR}}).

(3)

For any Hopf filtration
x
on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}[\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G]
stable under
\Gamma
there is a unique Hopf filtration on
K[\Res_{Z_{\textup{dR}}/K}G_{\textup{dR}}]
such that
x\mapsto x_{\textup{dR}}
via

\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B\;\cong\;\Res_{Z_{\textup{dR}}/K}G_{\textup{dR}}\times_{K}B.

Proof.

(1) The projection
\pi\colon G\to G/H
is
\Gamma
-equivariant and
H
-invariant, thus it induces an
H_{\textup{dR}}
-invariant morphism
\pi_{\textup{dR}}\colon G_{\textup{dR}}\to(G/H)_{\textup{dR}}
. By property of the quotient this factors through a morphism
G_{\textup{dR}}/H_{\textup{dR}}\to(G/H)_{\textup{dR}}
which is seen to be an isomorphism after extending scalars to
B
.

(2) The subgroup
(\rad G)_{\textup{dR}}
is connected normal and unipotent. Therefore we have an inclusion
\rad G)_{\textup{dR}}\subset\rad(G_{\textup{dR}})
which is seen to an equality after extending scalars to
B
.

(3) Write
H=\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
so that
H_{\textup{dR}}=\Res_{Z_{\textup{dR}}/K}G_{\textup{dR}}
. Let
\mathbb{Q}_{p}[H]^{\bullet}
be an Hopf filtration on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}[H]
stable under
\Gamma
. The morphisms
\mu
and
\nu
in the
definition

3.5
of an Hopf filtration are
\Gamma
-equivariant with respect to the action by conjugation of
\Gamma
on
H
. It follows that the filtration of
K[H_{\textup{dR}}]
defined by

K[H_{\textup{dR}}]^{\alpha}=\mathbb{Q}_{p}[H]^{\alpha}_{\textup{dR}},\hskip 10.00002pt\alpha\in\mathbb{Q},

is Hopf and it does the job by construction.
∎

Now we reset notation and work over
Z=\Spec\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. In this case crystalline actions on affine algebraic groups admit the following characterization:

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
G
be an affine algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\rho\colon\Gamma\to G
a group morphism. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

The action of
\Gamma
on
G
by left multiplication is crystalline.

(2)

The action of
\Gamma
on
G
by right multiplication is crystalline.

(3)

There exists a finite dimensional faithful representation
i\colon G\hookrightarrow\GL(V)
such that
i\circ\rho\colon\Gamma\to\GL(V)
is crystalline.

(4)

Given a representation
V
of
G
the induced linear action of
\Gamma
is crystalline.

If one of the preceding equivalent conditions holds, then the action of
\Gamma
on
G
by conjugation is crystalline.

Proof.

Conditions (1) and (2) are equivalent because the inversion map
G\to G
is
\Gamma
-equivariant when
\Gamma
acts by left multiplication on the source and by
(\gamma,g)\mapsto g\gamma^{-1}
on the target. The implication (4)
\Rightarrow(3)
is clear. The implication (2)
\Rightarrow
(4) follows from the fact that any representation of
G
is union of finite dimensional subrepresentations and any finite dimensional representation of
G
is isomorphic to a subrepresentation of some finite direct sum of the regular representation
[
Mil17
, cor. 4.13]
. For (3)
\Rightarrow
(2) notice that the closed embedding

i\colon G\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathbb{V}(\End V\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\hskip 10.00002ptg\longmapsto(g,\det g^{-1})

is
\Gamma
-equivariant when
\Gamma
acts on
\End V\oplus\det V^{\vee}
by
(f,\lambda)\mapsto(f\gamma^{-1},\det\gamma\lambda)
. For the last statement we already argued that the conjugation action is crystalline.
∎

We are ready to state and prove the main result of this section, which is the existence of a canonical Hopf filtration for an affine group with a crystalline action:

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
G
be an affine algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\rho\colon\Gamma\to G
a group morphism such that the action of
\Gamma
on
G
by right multiplication is crystalline. Then,

(1)

there exists a unique Hopf filtration
h_{G}
on
K[G_{\textup{dR}}]
such that for any representation
V
of
G
the Hodge filtration
h_{V}
on
V_{\textup{dR}}
is the one induced by
h_{G}
;

(2)

given a morphism of affine algebraic groups
f\colon G\to H
the action of
\Gamma
on
H
by right multiplication is crystalline and
h_{H}
is the filtration induced by
h_{G}
;

(3)

if
G
is reductive and
Q\subset G^{\circ}
is a parabolic subgroup normalized by
\Gamma
, then
h_{G}
is induced by a unique Hopf filtration
h_{Q}
on
K[Q_{\textup{dR}}]
and the Hopf filtration
h_{Q^{\textup{ss}}}
induced by
h_{Q}
via
Q_{\textup{dR}}\to Q_{\textup{dR}}^{\textup{ss}}=Q_{\textup{dR}}/\rad Q_{\textup{dR}}
is

h_{Q^{\textup{ss}}}=\pr_{Q}(h_{G}).

Proof.

(1) The uniqueness is clear: For any faithful representation
G\hookrightarrow\GL(V)
the induced map

\{\textup{Hopf filtrations on $K[G_{\textup{dR}}]$}\}\longrightarrow\{\textup{filtrations on $V_{\textup{dR}}$}\}

is injective by
proposition

3.7
. To show the existence we proceed in several steps:

Construction of the filtration.
Write
L
for
G
endowed with the action of
\Gamma
by left multiplication, and write
P=L_{\textup{dR}}
. The group law
G\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}L\to L
on
G
is equivariant with respect to the action of
\Gamma
by conjugation on
G
and by left multiplication on
L
. This induces an action

m\colon G_{\textup{dR}}\times_{K}P=G_{\textup{dR}}\times_{K}L_{\textup{dR}}\longrightarrow L_{\textup{dR}}=P

making
P
into a principal bundle under
G_{\textup{dR}}
. Moreover the actions of
\Gamma
on
L
given by
(\gamma,g)\mapsto\gamma g
and
(\gamma,g)\mapsto g\gamma^{-1}
commute. Therefore
P=L_{\textup{dR}}
inherits an action of
\Gamma
by
K
-schemes isomorphisms. We claim that this action of
\Gamma
on
P
is crystalline and

P_{\textup{dR}}=G_{\textup{dR}}.

Here and afterwards for any
K
-scheme
X=\Spec A
with a crystalline
K
-linear action of
\Gamma
we write
X_{\textup{dR}}=\Spec{(A\otimes_{K}B)^{\Gamma}}
as in
remark

5.9
. Once this claim is proved we set
h_{G}
to be the filtration on
K[G_{\textup{dR}}]
induced by the Hodge filtration on
P_{\textup{dR}}
via the above identification.

To prove the claim pick a faithful representation
i\colon G\hookrightarrow\GL(V)
. Then
i
is
\Gamma
-equivariant both for the actions of left and right multiplication. By looking first at the action by left multiplication, the morphism
i
induces a closed embedding

i\colon P=L_{\textup{dR}}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Iso_{K}(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}K,V_{\textup{dR}}).

Here for finite dimensional
K
-vector spaces
W
and
W^{\prime}
we write
\Iso_{K}(W,W^{\prime})
for the
K
-scheme whose points with values in a
K
-algebra
A
are isomorphisms of
A
-modules
W\otimes_{K}A\to W^{\prime}\otimes_{K}A
. Note that
P_{B}
is the orbit under composition by
G_{\textup{dR},B}
of the
B
-point of
\Iso_{K}(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}K,V_{\textup{dR}})
given by the canonical isomorphism

V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B\cong V_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{K}B.

Now the action of
\Gamma
on
G
and
\GL(V)
by multiplication on the right commutes with the action by multiplication on the left, therefore it induces an action of
\Gamma
on
P
and
\Iso_{K}(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}K,V_{\textup{dR}})
by
K
-scheme isomorphisms. Explicitly, the action on
\Gamma
on
\Iso_{K}(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}K,V_{\textup{dR}})
is just given via its linear action on
V
. In particular the morphism

\Iso_{K}(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}K,V_{\textup{dR}})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathbb{V}(H\oplus\det H^{\vee})\hskip 10.00002pt\textup{where $H=\Hom_{K}(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}K,V_{\textup{dR}})$}

is
\Gamma
-equivariant where
\Gamma
acts on the target via its linear action on
V
. This shows that the action of
\Gamma
on
P
is crystalline. The induced morphism
i
is
\Gamma
-equivariant with respect to these actions and therefore induces a closed embedding

i_{\textup{dR}}\colon P_{\textup{dR}}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Iso_{K}(V_{\textup{dR}},V_{\textup{dR}})=\GL(V_{\textup{dR}}).

Note that
P_{\textup{dR},B}
is the orbit under composition by
G_{\textup{dR},B}
of the identity in
\GL(V_{\textup{dR},B})
. In other words
P_{\textup{dR}}=G_{\textup{dR}}
as desired.

Hopf property.
Note that the morphism
m
is
\Gamma
-equivariant when
\Gamma
acts as above on
P
and trivially on
G_{\textup{dR}}
. The induced morphism

m_{\textup{dR}}\colon G_{\textup{dR}}\times_{K}P_{\textup{dR}}=G_{\textup{dR}}\times_{K}G_{\textup{dR}}\longrightarrow P_{\textup{dR}}=G_{\textup{dR}}

is the group law of
G_{\textup{dR}}
. Let
\mu\colon K[G_{\textup{dR}}]\to K[G_{\textup{dR}}]\otimes_{K}K[G_{\textup{dR}}]
be the
K
-algebra morphism induced by
m_{\textup{dR}}
. Since
\mu
is injective, the exactness of the Hodge filtration and its compatibility with tensor product imply

h^{\alpha}K[G_{\textup{dR}}]=\mu^{-1}(K[G_{\textup{dR}}]\otimes_{K}h^{\alpha}K[G_{\textup{dR}}])\hskip 10.00002pt\textup{for all $\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}$},

that is, the filtration
h_{G}
satisfies (
HF1
). To prove (
HF2
) consider the morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n\colon G_{\textup{dR}}\times_{K}G_{\textup{dR}}\times_{K}P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{\textup{dR}}\times_{K}P\times_{K}G_{\textup{dR}}\times_{K}P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\times_{K}P,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\times\mu}

where the first map is
(g,h,x)\mapsto(g,x,h,x)
and the second
(g,x,h,y)\mapsto(gx,hy)
.
The above composite morphism is
\Gamma
-equivariant when
\Gamma
acts on
P
as above and trivally on
N_{\textup{dR}}
. The morphism of
K
-algebras

\nu\colon K[G_{\textup{dR}}]\otimes K[G_{\textup{dR}}]\longrightarrow K[G_{\textup{dR}}]\otimes K[G_{\textup{dR}}]\otimes K[G_{\textup{dR}}]

induced by
n_{\textup{dR}}
via the equality
P_{\textup{dR}}=G_{\textup{dR}}
is the one appearing in (
HF2
) for
G_{\textup{dR}}
. Since
\nu
is injective, again the exactness of the Hodge filtration and its compatibility with tensor product imply

\displaystyle\nu^{-1}(K[G_{\textup{dR}}]\otimes K[G_{\textup{dR}}]\otimes h^{\alpha}K[G_{\textup{dR}}])

\displaystyle=h^{\alpha}(K[G_{\textup{dR}}]\otimes K[G_{\textup{dR}}])

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha=\beta+\gamma}h^{\beta}K[G_{\textup{dR}}]\otimes h^{\gamma}K[G_{\textup{dR}}],

for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}
. This concludes the proof that the filtration
h_{G}
is Hopf.

Universal property.
Any representation of
G
is the union of its finite dimensional subrepresentations so it suffices to prove the statement for any finite dimensional representation
\rho\colon G\to\GL(V)
. The morphism of schemes

\mathbb{V}(V^{\vee})\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}L\longrightarrow\mathbb{V}(V^{\vee}),\hskip 10.00002pt(f,g)\longmapsto fg

is
\Gamma
-equivariant when
\Gamma
acts on
\mathbb{V}(V^{\vee})\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}L
by
(f,g)\mapsto(f\gamma^{-1},\gamma g)
and trivially on
\mathbb{V}(V^{\vee})
. Therefore it induces a morphism of
K
-schemes

s\colon\mathbb{V}(V_{\textup{dR}}^{\vee})\times_{K}P=\mathbb{V}(V_{\textup{dR}}^{\vee})\times_{K}L_{\textup{dR}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{V}(V^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}K).

The above action of
\Gamma
on
\mathbb{V}(V^{\vee})\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}L
commutes with the action of
\Gamma
induced by right multiplication on
L
. Therefore
s
is
\Gamma
-equivariant when
\Gamma
acts on
P
as above, on
V^{\vee}
via the representation contragradient to
\rho
and trivially on
\mathbb{V}(V_{\textup{dR}}^{\vee})
. The induced morphism

s_{\textup{dR}}\colon\mathbb{V}(V_{\textup{dR}}^{\vee})\times_{K}G_{\textup{dR}}=\mathbb{V}(V_{\textup{dR}}^{\vee})\times_{K}P_{\textup{dR}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{V}(V_{\textup{dR}}^{\vee})

is the dual action of
G_{\textup{dR}}
on
V_{\textup{dR}}
. The morphism
s_{\textup{dR}}
is defined by the
K
-linear map

\sigma\colon V_{\textup{dR}}\longrightarrow V_{\textup{dR}}\otimes K[G_{\textup{dR}}]

defining the action of
N_{\textup{dR}}
on
V_{\textup{dR}}
. Since
\sigma
is injective, once again the exactness of the Hodge filtration and its compatibility with tensor product imply

h^{\alpha}V_{\textup{dR}}=\sigma^{-1}(V\otimes h^{\alpha}K[G_{\textup{dR}}]),\hskip 20.00003pt\textup{for all }\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}.

The right-hand side of the previous identity is by definition the filtration induced by the Hopf filtration
h_{V}
; see the discussion preceding
proposition

3.6
. Thus
h_{V}
is the filtration induced by
h_{G}
.

(2) Let
\sigma\colon H\hookrightarrow\GL(W)
be a faithful representation of
H
. Then
\sigma\circ f\circ\rho
is a crystalline representation and
\sigma
is
\Gamma
-equivariant for the right multiplication by
\Gamma
. It follows that the action by right multiplication of
\Gamma
on
H
is crystalline. Moreover
the induced map

\{\textup{Hopf filtrations on $K[H_{\textup{dR}}]$}\}\longrightarrow\{\textup{filtrations on $W_{\textup{dR}}$}\}

is injective by
proposition

3.7
. The Hopf filtration
h_{H}
and the filtration induced by
h_{G}
on
K[H_{\textup{dR}}]
have both image
h_{W}
, thus they coincide.

(3) Let
N\subset G
be the normalizer of the parabolic subgroup
Q
. Since
\Gamma
normalizes
Q
the image of
\rho
is contained in
N
. It follows from (2) that the filtrations
h_{G}
is induced by the Hopf filtration
h_{N}
of
K[N_{\textup{dR}}]
. Now
Q=N^{\circ}
thus the induced map

\{\textup{Hopf filtrations on $K[Q_{\textup{dR}}]$}\}\longrightarrow\{\textup{Hopf filtrations on $K[N_{\textup{dR}}]$}\}

is bijective by
proposition

3.7
. Therefore
h_{N}
comes from a Hopf filtration on
K[Q_{\textup{dR}}]
. The last statement follows from
remark

3.10
.
∎

5.3.
From étale to de Rham

The goal of this section is showing that
p
-adic Hodge theory transforms
\Gamma
-étale data into de Rham data. Let
Z
be a finite étale
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-scheme with an action of
\Gamma
. Suppose that the action is unramified and that

R:=\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{O}_{Z})

is a product of unramified extensions of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The
K
-scheme
Z_{\textup{dR}}
is finite étale over
K
by
lemma

5.5
and by
example

5.6
the
K
-algebra
R_{\textup{dR}}
is then a product of unramified extensions of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Definition 5.13
.

A
\Gamma
-étale datum
D=(V,G,\rho)
on
Z
is
transportable
if the representation
V
is crystalline and the image of
\rho\colon\Gamma\to\GL(V_{0})
is contained in a subgroup of
\GL(V_{0})
containing
\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
as a finite index subgroup.

When the representation
V
is crystalline, if the subgroup
G\subset\GL(V)
is of finite index in its normalizer, then
D
is transportable by
lemma

4.1
. Given a
\Gamma
-étale datum
(V,G,\rho)
on
Z
we let
\Gamma
act on
G
by conjugation and consider the group scheme
G_{\textup{dR}}
over
Z_{\textup{dR}}
.

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
D=(V,G,\rho)
be a transportable
\Gamma
-étale datum on
Z
. Then

D_{\textup{dR}}:=(V_{\textup{dR}},G_{\textup{dR}},\varphi_{V},h_{V})

is a de Rham datum on
Z_{\textup{dR}}
and the following properties hold:

(1)

For any filtration
x
on
D
there is a unique filtration
x_{\textup{dR}}
on
D_{\textup{dR}}
such that the isomorphism

\mathcal{B}(\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G,\Frac B)\cong\mathcal{B}(\Res_{Z_{\textup{dR}}/K}G_{\textup{dR}},\Frac B)
carries
x
to
x_{\textup{dR}}
;

(2)

For any grading
V^{(\bullet)}
on
D
there is a unique grading
V^{(\bullet)}_{\textup{dR}}
on
D_{\textup{dR}}
such that
the isomorphism
V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B\cong V_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{K}B
carries
V^{(\bullet)}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B
to
V^{(\bullet)}_{\textup{dR}}\otimes_{K}B
.

The above constructions are functorial and give rise to a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{transportable}\\
\textup{$\Gamma$-\'{e}tale data on $Z$}\end{array}\right\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{de Rham data on $Z_{\textup{dR}}$}\end{array}\right\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{filtered transportable}\\
\textup{$\Gamma$-\'{e}tale data on $Z$}\end{array}\right\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{filtered}\\
\textup{de Rham data on $Z_{\textup{dR}}$}\end{array}\right\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{graded transportable}\\
\textup{$\Gamma$-\'{e}tale data on $Z$}\end{array}\right\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\textup{graded}\\
\textup{de Rham data on $Z_{\textup{dR}}$}\end{array}\right\}}}
dR
forgetful
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr}
dR
forgetful
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr}
dR

where arrows in the above categories are taken to be isomorphisms.

Proof of
proposition

5.14
.

In order to prove that
D_{\textup{dR}}
is a de Rham datum we have to show that:

(a)
V_{\textup{dR}}
is a vector bundle over
Z_{\textup{dR}}
: This is
lemma

5.3
.

(b)
G_{\textup{dR}}\subset\GL(V_{\textup{dR}})
is a reductive group
Z_{\textup{dR}}
-scheme: Being reductive can be tested after extending scalars to
B
where the property is true because
G\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B
is reductive over
Z\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B
.

(c) the Frobenius operator
\varphi_{V}
on
V_{\textup{dR}}
is semilinear and normalizes
G_{\textup{dR}}
in the sense of
section

4.4
: Indeed
\varphi_{G}=\varphi_{\GL(V)\rvert G}
by functoriality of the construction of the Frobenius operator. On other hand
\varphi_{\GL(V)}
is the conjugation by
\varphi_{V}
.

(d) The Hodge filtration
h_{V}
on
V_{\textup{dR}}
belongs to
\mathcal{B}(G_{\textup{dR}},Z_{\textup{dR}})\subset\mathcal{B}(\GL(V_{\textup{dR}}),Z_{\textup{dR}})
: Since
D
is transportable there is a subgroup
N\subset\GL(V_{0})
containing the image of
\rho
and having
\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
as a subgroup of finite index, where
V_{0}
stands for
V
as
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector space. By
proposition

5.12
(1) the Hodge filtration
h_{V}
comes from a Hopf filtration
h_{N}
on
K[N_{\textup{dR}}]
. Now the group
N_{\textup{dR}}
is reductive and contains
\Res_{Z_{\textup{dR}}/K}G_{\textup{dR}}
as a subgroup of finite index. In particular the induced map

\mathcal{B}(G_{\textup{dR}},Z_{\textup{dR}}):=\mathcal{B}(\Res_{Z_{\textup{dR}}/K}G_{\textup{dR}},K)\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}(N_{\textup{dR}},K)

is bijective, so
h_{N}
comes from a necessarily unique point in
\mathcal{B}(G_{\textup{dR}},Z_{\textup{dR}})
.

Now statement (1) is
lemma

5.10
(3). For statement (2) note that by
example

5.6
any point
z\in Z_{\textup{dR}}
can be seen as a
\Gamma
-orbit
Z^{\prime}\subset Z
. The grading on
V_{\textup{dR}}
is then defined by

V_{\textup{dR},z}^{(\alpha)}:=\left(\sum_{z^{\prime}\in Z^{\prime}}V_{z^{\prime}}^{(\alpha)}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}B\right)^{\Gamma}

for
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}
. It is clear by
lemma

5.3
that isomorphisms of (filtered, graded)
\Gamma
-étale data are transformed into isomorphisms of (filtered, graded) de Rham data. The commutativity of the upper square is clear, while that of the lower one follows from the exactness of the functor
W\rightsquigarrow(W_{\textup{dR}},\varphi_{W},h_{W})
plus
lemma

5.10
(1) and (2) and
proposition

5.12
(3).
∎

Example 5.15
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an abelian scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
with generic fiber
A
and let
r\geqslant 1
be an integer nondivisibile by
p
. As in
example

4.3
we see
A[r](\bar{K})
as constant group scheme over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and consider its Cartier dual

Z:=A[r](\bar{K})^{\ast}=\Hom(A[r](\bar{K}),\mathbb{G}_{m}).

Since
r
is nondivisible by
p
the action of
\Gamma
on
Z
is unramified and
R=\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{O}_{Z})
is a product of unramified extensions of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Moreover

\Ker(\Gamma\to\Aut Z)=\Gal(\bar{K}/K^{\prime})

where
K^{\prime}\subset\bar{K}
is the unramified extension of
K
over which all
r
-torsion points are rational. Therefore
A[r]_{K^{\prime}}
is the constant group scheme with value
A[r](K^{\prime})
,

Z_{K^{\prime}}=A[r]^{\ast}_{K^{\prime}},

and by
example

5.6
we conclude that

Z_{\textup{dR}}=Z_{K^{\prime}}/\Gal(K^{\prime}/K)=A[r]^{\ast}.

Let
A^{\ast}
be the dual abelian variety and
A^{\natural}
the universal vector extension of
A^{\ast}
. The morphism
\pi\colon A^{\natural}\to A^{\ast}
of algebraic groups forgetting the connection on a line bundle induces an isomorphism

A^{\natural}[r]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}A^{\ast}[r]=A[r]^{\ast}=Z_{\textup{dR}}.

Let
E
be the
p
-adic étale local system of
example

4.3
and
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
the flat vector bundle of
example

4.10
on
A
. Then

E\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{A}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}\cong\mathcal{E}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

where the superscript ‘
ét
’ stands for the associated étale sheaf. Via this isomorphism the connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{E}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}
is induced by the derivation on
\mathcal{O}_{A}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}
. Let
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{A}
be a morphism of finite type
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-schemes with
\mathcal{X}
smooth of relative dimension
d
and generic fiber
X=\mathcal{X}_{K}
. By
[
Fal89
, th. 5.6]
we have an isomorphism

\textup{H}^{d}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X_{\bar{K}},f^{\ast}E)_{\textup{dR}}\cong\textup{H}^{d}_{\textup{dR}}(X/K;f^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},\nabla))

of vector bundles over
Z_{\textup{dR}}
compatible with the Poincaré pairing. In other words the
\Gamma
-étale datum
D=(V,G,\rho)
on
Z
of
example

4.3
is transportable because
G\subset\GL(V)
coincides with its normalizer and the de Rham datum
D_{\textup{dR}}
on
Z_{\textup{dR}}
is isomorphic to that in
example

4.10
.

Remark 5.16
.

In the above example the groups
\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G
and
\Res_{Z_{\textup{dR}}/K}G_{\textup{dR}}
are not isomorphic in general, even when
K=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Indeed, by looking at constant functions on the central torus, we can recover from those two groups the étale
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebras
\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{O}_{Z})
and
\Gamma(Z_{\textup{dR}},\mathcal{O}_{\textup{dR}})
respectively. These two
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebras are not isomorphic in general. This happens already for
r=2
,
p\neq 2
and
A
an elliptic curve over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with
A[2](\mathbb{Q}_{p})=0
. However, in
[
LS20
]
the two groups are implicitly identified, leading to issues with lemmas 5.49, 7.10 and 8.10 in loc. cit.

5.4.
Positivity from global purity

Recall that a number field
F
is CM if it is a totally imaginary quadratic extension of a totally real number field
F_{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
K
be a number field. If
K
contains a CM subfield, then there is a largest one
K^{\textup{CM}}\subset K
. Following
[
LV20
, 2.4]
a place
v
of
K
is called
friendly
if
v
is unramified and, in case
K
contains a CM subfield,
v
lies above a place of
(K^{\textup{CM}})_{\mathbb{R}}
which is inert in
K^{\textup{CM}}
. Let
v
be a
p
-adic place of
K
,
K_{v}
the completion of
K
at
v
,
\bar{K}_{v}
an algebraic closure of
K_{v}
and
\bar{K}
the algebraic closure of
K
in
\bar{K}_{v}
. We have a natural inclusion

\Gamma_{v}=\Gal(\bar{K}_{v}/K_{v})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Gamma=\Gal(\bar{K}/K).

Let
Z
be a finite étale
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-scheme such that
R:=\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{O}_{Z})
is a product of unramified extensions of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. We assume that
Z
is equipped with a continuous action of
\Gamma
and that the induced action of
\Gamma_{v}
is unramified. Given a
\Gamma
-étale datum on
Z
we obtain a
\Gamma_{v}
-étale datum by restriction of the underlying representations; hence in the crystalline case we get an associated de Rham datum on
Z_{\textup{dR}}
by
proposition

5.14
. For a point
z\in Z_{\textup{dR}}
we denote by
k_{z}
the residue field of
z
.

Proposition 5.17
.

Suppose
v
is friendly and
let
D=(V,G,\rho,x)
be a filtered
\Gamma
-étale datum with
G\subset\GL(V)
of finite index in its normalizer. Assume that the underlying representation is pure of some weight, and crystalline at all
p
-adic places. Let
D_{\textup{dR}}=(V_{\textup{dR}},G_{\textup{dR}},\varphi,h,x_{\textup{dR}})
be the associated de Rham datum at
v
. Then there exists
z\in Z_{\textup{dR}}
such that

h_{z}\in\mathcal{B}(G_{\textup{dR},z},k_{z})\hskip 10.00002pt\text{is positive with respect to}\hskip 10.00002pt\Stab_{G_{\textup{dR},z}}(x_{\textup{dR},z})^{\circ}.

Proof.

Let
Q=\Res_{Z/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\Stab_{G}(x)
be the Weil restriction of the stabilizer of
x
.
Since
x
is fixed under the action of
\Gamma
, the representation
\rho\colon\Gamma\to\GL(V_{0})
takes values in the normalizer
N
of the subgroup
Q\subset\GL(V_{0})
, where
V_{0}
stands for
V
seen as a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector space.
The action of
N
by conjugation on
Q
preserves its connected component and its unipotent radical. Therefore the adjoint action of
Q
on the Lie algebra
W:=\Lie\rad Q
extends to a representation

\sigma\colon N\longrightarrow\GL(W).

The composite representation
\sigma\circ\rho\colon\Gamma\to\GL(W)
is pure of weight zero: Indeed, it is a subrepresentation of the adjoint representation
\End(V_{0})
of
\Gamma
, and the latter is pure of weight zero because
V_{0}
is pure of some weight. Note that the restriction of
D
to
\Gamma_{v}
is transportable because
G\subset\GL(V)
is of finite index in its normalizer. Since the place
v
is friendly, it then follows by
[
LV20
, lemma 2.9]
that the weight of the Hodge filtration
h_{W}
induced by
p
-adic Hodge theory on
W_{\textup{dR}}=\Lie\rad Q_{\textup{dR}}

is

(5.1)

\wt(h_{W})=0.

By
proposition

5.12
(1) the filtrations
h=h_{V}
and
h_{W}
are both induced by the Hopf filtration
h_{N}
on
K[N_{\textup{dR}}]
. By
proposition

5.12
(3) the Hopf filtration on
K[N^{\textup{ss}}_{\textup{dR}}]
induced by
h_{N}
is

\pr_{Q^{\circ}_{\textup{dR}}}(h_{V})

where
N^{\textup{ss}}_{\textup{dR}}=N_{\textup{dR}}/\rad Q_{\textup{dR}}
: Indeed
Q^{\circ,\textup{ss}}_{\textup{dR}}=Q^{\circ}_{\textup{dR}}/\rad Q_{\textup{dR}}
is a subgroup of finite index in
N_{\textup{dR}}^{\textup{ss}}
hence Hopf filtrations on Hopf algebras of these groups can be identified by
proposition

3.7
. Now the representation
\sigma
is
\Gamma
-equivariant and the induced morphism

\sigma_{\textup{dR}}\colon N_{\textup{dR}}\longrightarrow\GL(W_{\textup{dR}})

is the adjoint representation. Its determinant
\det\sigma_{\textup{dR}}
factors through the modular character

\delta\colon N_{\textup{dR}}^{\textup{ss}}\longrightarrow\GL(\det W_{\textup{dR}}).

Therefore (
5.1
) implies

\langle\pr_{Q^{\circ}_{\textup{dR}}}(h_{V}),\delta\rangle=\wt(h_{W})=0.

In other words the point
h\in\mathcal{B}(G_{\textup{dR}},Z_{\textup{dR}})
is positive with respect to the parabolic subgroup

Q_{\textup{dR}}^{\circ}=\prod_{z\in Z_{\textup{dR}}}\Res_{k_{z}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\Stab_{G_{\textup{dR},z}}(x_{\textup{dR},z})^{\circ}.

Hence there is
z\in Z_{\textup{dR}}
such that
h_{z}\in\mathcal{B}(G_{z},k_{z})
is positive with respect to the parabolic subgroup
\Res_{k_{z}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\Stab_{G_{\textup{dR},z}}(x_{\textup{dR},z})^{\circ}
; see
example

3.14
. By
example

3.15
this is equivalent to saying that
h_{z}
is positive with respect to
\Stab_{G_{\textup{dR},z}}(x_{\textup{dR},z})^{\circ}
which concludes the proof.
∎

6.
Combinatorics on flag varieties

In this section we discuss a dimension estimate on orbits of flag varieties that will allow us to apply the Bakker-Tsimerman theorem later on. Let
k
be a perfect field.

6.1.
The adjoint polygon

Let
x\in\mathcal{B}(\GL(V),k)
where
V
is a
k
-vector space of dimension
d
, and let
F^{\bullet}V
be the corresponding filtration. Let
\alpha_{1}>\cdots>\alpha_{n}
denote the rational numbers for which
\gr^{\alpha_{i}}V\neq 0
. The
polygon
of
x
is the continuous piecewise affine function
\upsilon_{x}\colon[0,d]\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\upsilon_{x}(0)=0
and, for
i=1,\dots,n
, the slope of
\upsilon_{x}
on the interval
[\dim F^{\alpha_{i-1}}V,\dim F^{\alpha_{i}}V]
is
\alpha_{i}
where
\dim F^{\alpha_{0}}V=0
. The function
\upsilon_{x}
is by definition concave and satisfies
\upsilon_{x}(d)=d\wt(x)
,
\upsilon_{cx}=c\upsilon_{x}
for all rational numbers
c\geqslant 0
,
\upsilon_{gx}=\upsilon_{x}
